ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u5	Thank you very much, Madam Secretary. We proceed to the examination of item 1 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on the Research Network: Łukasiewicz (print No. 2148). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Science and Higher Education of Mr Piotr Dardziński to present the justification for the bill. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present a draft law on the Research Network: Łukasiewicz. This project is the implementation of the Law and Justice Programme and the exposé of Prime Minister Morawiecki. Its task is to achieve three main objectives. The first is to consolidate the potential of Polish institutes, which support the Polish economy in this context, and enable entrepreneurs to exploit the potential of Polish science to build competitive advantages in the global economy. The second objective is to enable the development of an environment in which it is possible to develop career paths of researchers who are involved in application research, otherwise called applied research or dedicated research, i.e. those that end in implementation. And the third objective is to institutionally strengthen research institutes and enable them to transform so that they can also compete in the acquisition of R &amp; D funds from European projects. The main objective of the bill is the establishment of a network, which will be managed by the Research Network Centre: Łukasiewicz. The Centre will have the status of research institute, research network institute: Łukasiewicz, as will all other research institutes whose legal status will be transformed into research network institute: Łukasiewicz. Here's a quick comment. A new concept for the development of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz has been prepared with a very extensive consideration of the comments of the social partners. From the original version, which provided for the construction of a single organisation, we moved on to the version in which we build the network. This means that in the first case research institutes would lose legal personality, while in the network they retain this subjectivity, only their status and name change. By the way, I would like to mention what kind of adjustments we have made, taking into account social demands. We have taken much more into account, stressed the scientific status of institutes and made it possible to extend the implementation of career paths for employees. We have also extended the scope of research, not only to basic research, i.e. not only to research which is dedicated research. As I mentioned earlier, the law was prepared for almost 2 years in close cooperation with academic communities, Polish entrepreneurs and Polish universities. The project, which was presented to the High House, obtained the far-reaching consent of all these communities as a project that makes it possible in a real way to exploit the potential of Polish science and creates a place that will not only create new technologies but also prepare, educate and attract young, ambitious scientists. In view of the above, I ask the High House to adhere to our project. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u7	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Ewa Malik, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u8	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present an opinion on the Government's draft law on the Research Network: Łukasiewicz, print No. 2148. The draft law provides for the creation of an integrated network of research organisations aimed at achieving their own objectives, whose activities will be coordinated by the central unit of Mon. Łukasiewicz Center. The main objective of this entity's activities will be to carry out research and R &amp; D relevant to the implementation of the economic, scientific, scientific and technological and innovative policies of the country, as well as to transfer knowledge and implement the results of research and development to the economy. The bill responds to the challenge of effective implementation of the objectives of the Responsible Development Strategy by 2020 (with a view to 2030) based on the Polish science and economy. A comprehensive reform of the Polish science system in 2010, which was to introduce a new quality and contribute to an increase in the level of research carried out in research institutes and, above all, to facilitate cooperation of science with industry, has failed because to this day few of these intentions have been achieved. The State's supervision of research institutes so far tended to control the formal correctness of public spending - as a result of research institutes, instead of cooperating with the government on the economic policy pursued and on the direction of the country's economic strategy, they defined for themselves the challenges and directions of development, so their aim was to maximise their own revenues rather than contribute to the development of the Polish economy. Economic policy of the Government of the Republic of Poland adopted in the document: a strategy for responsible development, in short: the SOR, presents very precisely strategic sectors and sectors, with proposed flagship projects, around which public intervention is to focus, and in particular the activities of research institutes. The SOR also identifies the necessary changes to the organisational and legal framework of the innovation system, one of which is the improvement of knowledge transfer mechanisms to the business sector and the creation of a matrix-based network of research centres based on existing research institutes. In view of the necessity to carry out the tasks indicated in the SOR, the government presented in print no. 2148 the project of creating the Łukasiewicz network. The network is intended to be a matrix-based, integrated and target-oriented organisation, created by the inclusion of parts of present-day research institutes with the right potential to achieve the purpose of the network. The network as a structure will have significant autonomy, but it must ensure more effective cooperation between research institutes that will be part of it, including. by harmonising financial management mechanisms, human resources, real estate or intellectual property rights. The network will also allow effective supervision of the activities of the integrated institutes. The network is to be an efficient technological and intellectual public administration, a kind of bridge between science and the economy. For the sake of the development of the Polish economy and the implementation of the strategy for responsible development, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice recommends that the High Sejm adopt a draft law on the Research Network: Łukasiewicz, print No. 2148, for further proceedings in the Sejm committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u9	Thank you, Member. Mr Arkadiusz Carrot, Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u10	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! The objective of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz is to conduct applied and industrial research and development, as well as transfer of knowledge and implementation of research results or support the economic policy of the state. In the opinion of the Civic Platform club prepared by the Ministry of Science and Higher Education, the project has many disadvantages and will generally not serve the development of Polish science or the effective implementation of research results into the economy. It should be noted that the construction of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz storms the existing achievements and scientific status of institutes that will become part of it. After entry into force of the proposed law, these institutions will lose the status of the research unit, international financing, as well as powers, among others. to give degrees to a doctor or a doctor who is habilitated. In addition, there are other arguments demonstrating the weaknesses of this project. Firstly, the project records seek to limit the autonomy of research and science units. The project provides that 38 leading Polish research institutes will be placed under the supervision of one institution - Centrum Łukasiewicz. It will impose research projects on institutes that have worked alone so far. In fact, it will be central management and the State will exercise control over all their activities. Moreover, the management of the new research network will be imposed from the top, which will take away the autonomy of these entities. Secondly, the project deprives the institutes of their existing competence to award degrees and various types of certificates, including in particular international ones. The staff of the institutes, forced into one network, will lose the status of the scientific staff. Thirdly, the draft law questions the future of those research institutes that have not been included in the network, and there are as many as 76 of them. As part of the drafting of the bill, a broad consultation was carried out with all stakeholders, resulting in 164 critical comments. Of the many objections raised, only a few were taken into account. In the course of the consultation, among others, they were accused. centralisation, increased bureaucracy, inappropriate concept, loss of existing achievements, powers and certificates, little chance of synergy in business. Moreover, the absence of competitions for the position of the president of the Centre and the directors of the Network Institutes, which shows that there is no independence and dependence on the Minister of these Institutes. This means centralisation rather than strengthening the subjectivity of these institutes. The establishment of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz is more like a demonstration to demonstrate that the strategy for responsible development by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki is being implemented. Only that this implementation is based on sham moves. Instead of doing research, scientists will be engaged in reorganization of the entities in which they work. Is there another way to strengthen these research institutes? It can be said that a further gradual reform of research institutes and a possible combination of units of similar research matter is needed. It seems that the amendment of the Law of 30 April 2010 on Research Institutes would be a much better solution, as indicated in its position by the General Council of Research Institutes. Chapter 8a could be added to the existing provisions, which would enable the Minister, at his request or at the request of the directors of supervised institutes, to set up such institutes. This would definitely be a better solution. On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I would like to say that the project contains many flaws, and at this stage it is not possible for the Civic Platform Club to accept it in this form, so at the later stage of the project process we will propose amendments to it. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u12	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! There is no doubt that this bill will be passed. Members of the Law and Justice will vote in accordance with the directives to be presented to them. In general, I would like to say that I am wondering what the minister, the supposed model deregulation, is doing, that he is introducing another research solution, which these researches in a certain level are even more centralized than ever before. Perhaps it is a reflection that the solutions behind which he was walled and criticized in previous legislations did not really meet, how honestly he writes about it in his justification, expectations. But wouldn't it be better if the applicant listened to the clues and the law on research institutes, the one in 2016, improved and the number of these institutes was significantly reduced rather than introducing further legislation? On this occasion, the applicant performs an interesting PR manipulation. It does not mention at all that the amendment introduced by print No 2148 also refers to the 2016 Law on Research Institutes. It refers only to earlier legislation in which the applicant also participated and which was to be exemplary. The 2016 Act is not discussed at all, but in the justification of the present law, the applicant uses almost the same argument that we presented at the time, but this argument is not used to remedy the existing reality, but for its own purposes, which is contrary to the use of phraseology in the justification and the law itself, the strict submission of certain research institutes to the Minister of Science and Higher Education. This is so interesting that research institutes are formally and substantively subject to as many as 16 ministers responsible for specific departments of the national economy. In general, the establishment of the Łukasiewicz network is intended, as is apparent from the Act, to focus the activities of scientific institutes entering the network on efficiency in the form of concrete implementation into the economy, but these institutes are deprived of their powers in the field of doctorisation and habilitation. This is not a bad thing, as the 2016 Law on Research Institutes significantly reduced their scientific rank, even by undermining scientific requirements for the Institute's Director, as well as the Scientific Council, but the adopted solution makes the Institutes equally weak scientific, remaining outside the Łukasiewicz network, still have the right to doctorate and habilitation. Draft Law on the Research Network: Łukasiewicz is also in many places contrary to the prepared law on higher education, even with regard to the minimum staff. It follows from the Act that universities working with a networked institute will be able to include employees of the cooperating institute as a minimum. First of all, the new law plans to eliminate the minimum staff, but also the applicant - o tempora, o mores! - has used an interesting solution if this minimum staff is preserved. He calculated that two doctors of the research institute were sufficient for one doctor-habilitated or titled professor employed at a university working with the Institute of the Łukasiewicz network to keep this minimum staff. This bill is generally redundant. And if it is necessary, as the applicants want it, they simply acknowledge that the current model of supervision of individual ministers over their institutes has not been proven and that the money spent on the activities of both ministers and their institutes has been wasted. The applicants throw not stones, but a boulder into the backyard of the government, which has been running research institutes for more than two years. In fact, they are simply writing: so far the supervision of ministers of research institutes was usually reduced to checking the formal correctness of public spending. As a result, the system as a whole has been faced with inefficiencies in the use of material and human resources or duplication of investments and areas of activity. The solution is wrong, for example, because. tax write-offs are not foreseen for revenue from the sale of intellectual property. In conclusion, this legislation is redundant. [[♪ The bell ♪]] It's creating another unnecessary law. Thank you very much.

On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the government draft law on the Research Network: Łukasiewicz. The applicants in the explanatory memorandum stated that the creation of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz is the answer to the challenges facing the Polish economy and science. The establishment of the network is intended to support the implementation of the Responsible Development Strategy. It seems that the presented diagnosis leaves no illusions. The current situation is unsatisfactory, the achievements of institutes leave much to be desired. The individual successes and effective activities of some institutes do not change this assessment. There are currently 114 research institutes that carry out research in almost all areas of science. The result of this change is better, more effective cooperation between research institutes, among others. better use of resources, employees, finance and real estate. Effectiveness today is not served by very fragmented supervision of research institutes, which is carried out by 16 ministers. The applicants cite a number of arguments to demonstrate the inefficient activities of the institutes. Institutes are unable to achieve economies of scale, resulting in a small number of large and valuable projects. The revenues of research institutes from R &amp; D services, which should be a key aspect of their activities, should be considered unsatisfactory, whereas these revenues are 7%. In the case of 37 research institutes, real estate rental revenues are higher than revenues from the sale of services and 32 institutes have not obtained a single patent. Research institutes are largely not sufficiently competitive at international level. In 2015, 35 of them did not receive any funding from international grants and nine received only small resources from this source. Moreover, the situation in the current perspective under Horizon 2020 is not better. So will the bill submitted by the government change this adverse situation? Do the solutions proposed in the draft law give hope for achieving the objectives? Will the reasoning cited in the explanatory memorandum make it possible to create conditions for the effective operation of institutes? As I have already mentioned, applicants point out that the key elements are support for the implementation of the Responsible Development Strategy, as well as the existing strategic sectors and sectors with flagship programmes around which public intervention is to be concentrated. This project is about to strengthen this process. I must say that the analysis of this project does not offer such hope. I've analysed the records, or actually the slides of Prime Minister Morawiecki, I've checked the pillars he's talking about, the flagship projects that are being introduced, I've compared them to the field of activity of the various institutes, and this analysis didn't work out well, Minister. I would like to say that it is difficult to find in the network institutes dealing with medical devices, telemedicine, specialist software, mining systems, high quality food, military systems. Half of the priority sectors are not reflected in the activities of the institutes forming part of the network. What about the other institutes? You are analyzing 114 institutes, while you should do so in relation to the 38 that will go to the new research network. This analysis has not been carried out. It seems that once again, the slides have been unbelievably scattered with reality. We will not, of course, be against this project at this stage, in order to give the Ministry a chance to be open to changes in committees, but today it is difficult to imagine that we will have synergies if only de facto development institutes are introduced into the network. How to achieve synergies e.g. between the Institute of Wood Technology, the Institute of Electronic Materials Technology and the Institute of Mining Machinery? These institutes are extremely scattered, and in addition the powers of the President are gigantic, in particular in the field of property management, and de facto the possibility of their free formation is unlimited, [[♪ The bell ♪]] including sales without the consent of other partners. As I said, the Modern Posel Club does not apply for rejection, but hopes for a major revision of the project during the committee work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Why? This is because they conduct coordinated policies using the economies of scale of individual institutes. It was not in Poland, it was missing in Poland. The Institutes also made one very important request concerning the lack of state capacity, the lack of public administration's capacity to define key projects and key areas. Today, the government is changing this situation. The Responsible Development Strategy defines areas that are central to the government and the Ministry of Science... Here I would like to draw your attention to the fact that the bill submitted is prepared by two ministries, by the Minister for Science and Higher Education and, even earlier, by the Minister for Development. This is the best example of eliminating silos in the government and coordinating economic policy and scientific policy. So we respond, firstly, to the request of the institutes themselves to increase, consolidate institutes so that they can compete globally, and secondly, we respond to their request to define precisely what areas will be key to the government and in which areas these institutes should develop their competences. I would like to draw your attention, because with regard to the charge of centralisation, which has appeared in the statements of many ladies and gentlemen, it is important to see the proportion. In the Research Network: Łukasiewicz will find about 8 thousand. employees. Of those 8,000. employees 1,5 thousand. has the status of scientific staff, about 4.5 thousand. they are laboratory staff. It's about $1.5,000, and you're talking about us building a central communist unit? In Polish science 150 thousand work. academic staff. Where's the centralization moment? What do we do? A specialized group of scientists, who are particularly interested in working with the economy, are presenting the framework within which this cooperation can be used effectively. In this sense, if you ask whether these institutes will have the right to doctorate, I answer that this particular request did not raise much doubt in the public consultation. This is one of the points that we have come to as soon as possible simply to an agreement with the social partners. Why? Because in research institutes already today, most researchers working there are primarily focused on doing projects that are technologically advanced and interesting from the point of view of the economy, which does not mean that they do not want to develop their scientific career, but they want to develop it based, symbolically speaking, on implementation rather than on publications. So what does the Research Network: Łukasiewicz build? Research Network: Łukasiewicz differentiates career path systems in Poland. It doesn't centralize them, it just differentiates them. Basic research researchers working on theoretical problems can develop their careers in higher education and in higher education institutions. They can develop them in the PAN institutes. They can also develop them in other research institutes. In the Institutes of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz we will focus on implementation, not theoretical research. This is the consequence of the policy adopted, which was also advocated by the institutes themselves. So we are not dealing with any centralisation within the system and we are not dealing with centralization within the system. The organizational culture, which we proposed in this act, is that Centrum Łukasiewicz is an entity supporting institutes. [[It's a corporate deal.]] She shapes corporate governance. Thank you very much, exactly, for your attention. All your comments about the fact that there is a breach of the autonomy of subordinate entities are inconsistent with the assumptions which are legally described in this law. So how will Łukasiewicz work and why Centrum Łukasiewicz... One of your fellow Members asked that question. If you'll excuse me, I took a quick note, and I don't want to waste any more time searching for those fragments. But of course I will try to answer all the questions. If I missed any of the questions, of course we will answer them in writing. [[Law 2.0.]] Exactly. [[January 23rd meeting.]] How will this corporate governance work in the centre of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz and why will Centrum Łukasiewicz - Mr Łukasiewicz asked me - also have research powers? Centrum Łukasiewicz will also be a research institute, because in the process of commercialising knowledge apart from technology we need knowledge and research on how to commercialize in general. Two examples: we should conduct far-reaching research on how to protect intellectual property, and we should conduct far-reaching research on how to analyze global markets and build business models when it comes to placing our technology and supporting Polish entrepreneurs. If I ask a Member today, who of the Technology Institutes is involved in these areas, if I ask one of the directors whether they are involved in these areas, then the answer is natural to them: This is not our area of competence. We are institutes that deal with robotics, pharmaceuticals, biotechnology. We don't do economic analysis, we don't do marketing analysis, we don't do legal analysis. And in this sense, the centre complements the structure of this system of commercialization of research with competences that are simply not in other institutes. The Centre of Łukasiewicz - I make this declaration publicly - will have no ambition in any way to duplicate the scope of research that exists today in institutes, and the directors of institutes know it. Because the structure is based on autonomous research institutes and an existing centre, in a natural way in the so-called. The matrix management system will be different types of projects. There will be projects to be carried out by individual institutes for which autonomy and decision on how to cooperate, who to cooperate with, to what extent it will belong solely to the Director. They will exist - and it is the network that gives this opportunity - projects that are carried out at the level of the network, i.e. with different institutes. Corporate governance will allow several institutes to carry out one project, and there will be no problem... Today's the problem. Even with who is to be the leader of this project. And the third thing: there will also be projects whose scale will be so large that they will involve the entire network, and then their coordinator will be the centre. So in short, as in the normal corporate governance, the decision structure is diversified. She defines what responsibilities are, as well as responsibilities. So the center will not lead - as it is written in the Act - the current supervision... It means that it will not run the day-to-day management of research projects in individual institutes. It will be a matter of competence of directors and possibly coordinators of interinstitutional projects, i.e. so-called. Project managers. The Centre merely ensures that projects do not duplicate, exclude each other and effectively exploit both the infrastructure potential and the human resources potential. Now, about centralization -- you asked me that -- I don't know if you've noticed that we're in a difficult situation. On the one hand, we are criticized for centralizing, although, as we said, there are only 38. On the number of employees I have shown you that we could really say that we are doing some kind of piloting on how science can function differently than we are building monoculture for the whole scientific community. On the other hand, you say it's not enough, and there's still a bit of a demand somewhere, and the question is, what about research institutes that are off the grid. Now is the question: Is it that we are now diversifying - in our opinion it is good that we are creating various systems - or would you rather we continue to close this system, that is, to make one formula of the institute? Exactly, the research institutes that worked in the current formula will work in the existing formula. And please pay attention to how beautiful it will be: in two years' time we will be able to make a summary of how institutes are developing under the current law on research institutes - most of the country insists on leaving it with small, small corrections - and we will be able to compare with this the effectiveness of managing institutes that are in the Research Network: Łukasiewicz. If it turns out that this model is suitable for economic development, we will be able to scale it into more institutes. We're very wise. How did we select institutes that enter the Research Network: Łukasiewicz? As I mentioned earlier, the law is prepared by two ministries: there is commercialisation of research, the network is intended to serve the economy, so the minister of the economy is involved, and because it is a science area, the minister of science is involved. To all the other ministers who have institutes under their supervision, we have sent a proposal to connect these institutes to the network. The Institutes, in consultation with the supervising ministers, or actually ministers after consulting here, decided themselves whether they were joining institutes on the network, without any, and here somewhere such a formulation, coercion, fell. Even here it was stronger: that it was somehow by force, by force they were incarnated, said Mr Arkadiusz Marchewka, by force and by compulsion that scientists and directors were incorporated into the Research Network: Łukasiewicz. I understand rhetoric and metaphoricalism, but the facts were that joining the research network was decided apart from the Minister of Development, the Minister of Energy and the Minister of Digitization. We considered that this is the potential that is sufficient to start supporting projects implemented within the framework of a responsible development strategy. The selection process of institutes was therefore very free, the structure of the management of these institutes is properly in line with corporate governance commonly used in the management of large projects. We're catching up, and we need to do this fast, because we don't have time to catch up with the world in how it reorganizes its structure. We would like to build a third largest research network in Europe so that we can finally start competition for grants and funds from the 2020 horizon and deliver technologies to Polish entrepreneurs. Mr Owa talked about the fact that one does not fit the other, that there is no competence of research institutes in what is included in the SOR. Yes, because we do not centralize, we do not claim that only institutes in the Research Network: Łukasiewicz will implement the SOR. Various institutes will implement the SOR, as well as universities will implement some research areas. Institutes in the Research Network: Łukasiewicz will implement only part of key projects, because they do not build monoculture, they do not build an omnipotent research network. And it's natural. The question of the Member was also very interesting because, when asking what one institute has in common with another institute, he asked according to the classic logic of division of disciplines, division of silos and division of industries. Today we have most of the corporations that are interbranch, we have definitely corporations that are cross-sectoral, and we also have research that is naturally interdisciplinary. You would be surprised at how much these institutes have in common and how sometimes even the names of institutes are confusing about what these institutes do. And I assure you that there will be a huge field of interdisciplinaryness within Lukasiewicz and that these institutes will know how to exploit their potential. Another quite often mentioned charge was the charge... excuse me, a remark concerning the status of research network institutes: Łukasiewicz. In this context, the State was very often using the wording "research entity'. This is an issue that has also taken us a long time in discussions, in consultation with the social partners. The term "research entity' is not a legal formulation; it does not exist in any law. The call not to deprive Łukasiewicz Institutes of the status of a research unit is an appeal which cannot be met because there is no status of a research entity, but there is a status of research institute, which is regulated by the relevant law. In this sense, the Institutes of Łukasiewicz, because they come out of the regime of the Law on Research Institutes, enter into the regime of the Law on Research Network: Łukasiewicz. It could be said otherwise - from the fact that the PAS institutes are not research institutes, it does not appear that they are not scientific bodies. Okay? They are the same scientific units. In this sense, the Research Network: Łukasiewicz... Mr Marchewka was gracious to quote a passage from the article. 1. We could ask ourselves what is an organization that has the following objectives. I quote: The goals of the Network are: 1) conducting applied and industrial research and development and, where appropriate, basic research, including defence (...), where point 2 refers to knowledge transfer and implementation of research and development results. All of Łukasiewicz is a science dedicated to the economy. To answer the question of who will select and who will decide, and why there is a certain reservation in the case of basic research... is this However, the concession is to take into account the environmental comments submitted in the public consultation - we have added basic research in justified cases, they are so-called. basic research dedicated, i.e. we are testing in theory whether something in the future may be the subject of research, which will already be part of implementation research. If by definition, we know from the assumption that the objective of basic research it is not a implementation, it is Łukasiewicz who is not the best place to conduct this type of research, but, as I have shown earlier, there are a lot of these places. In order to reassure you about the scientific status, rights, obligations and powers connected with holidays, for example, - all the existing powers of research staff of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz remains actually in the form that applies to research institutes. On the other hand, the Research Network: Łukasiewicz after the adoption of the law, because this is not possible before its adoption, will be entered into the Polish education and science system in the proper law - either in this law, which is still in force, or in the future law, which will be prepared. You also asked about the fate of research institutes, i.e. those institutes that do not enter the Research Network: Łukasiewicz. In part, I have mentioned that this is a conscious choice, a conscious decision to diversify the situation of institutes. So that after the public consultation, we made adjustments to the project. Some of the comments made by Members concerned the fact that we are taking financing from those institutes that are off-line. The funding of institutes that do not enter the Research Network: Łukasiewicz, remains and will be paid on the existing rules, so nothing changes here. I hope I've gotten back to... There was also the question that the deprivation of this status makes it impossible to apply for international grants. In a natural way, because there is no deprivation of the status of the individual who conducts the research, actually access to grants will be significantly greater thanks to the economies of scale. I also said about the role of the center, I think about degrees and academic titles as well. So let me finally make a brief reference to this - although Mr Rzoniec has already preceded me - why Łukasiewicz. [[I'm here, I'm here.]] Yes, you're an MP. Why Lukasiewicz? I mean, we're all still at school... Lukasiewicz, as Mr Rzońc mentioned, taught Texas oil workers how to drill and how oil should be processed. Lukasiewicz was then a tycoon in the oil trade in Galicia, because it was naturally a time when Poland did not exist. He produced the best quality distillates, paid his employees the highest rate and had the lowest prices, and made a lot of money, he was a very wealthy person. [[Applause]] And we would very much like the Polish economy to earn money in science and Polish scientists to earn money in science so that they can then... Here is the third part of the answer, why Łukasiewicz. Because Lukasiewicz, after making huge money, was one of the biggest patrons, founded some of the first schools for girls, funded bridges, funded roads, funded social assistance, or was someone who made the invention, commercialized this invention globally, actually created a new business industry, made huge money and was able to share it. And that's why we chose to be patron of Łukasiewicz. We chose to be patron of Łukasiewicz, because he best suits what is included in the plan of responsible development. He answers that science should serve people, including those who cultivate it. And those who cultivate it should benefit from it and be able to share it. Therefore, once again, I ask the High House to favour this law and I hope that it will continue to be proceded. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The network is also intended to support public administration with highly specialised expertise in the area of new technologies. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u90	Dear Marshal, High Seym! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to express my opinion on the draft law on government departments. The applicant's explanatory memorandum states that the project pursues two objectives. The first is the ordering of public spaces and the second is the efficient coordination of measures related to the housing policy of the State. It is not necessary to convince anyone to care for public space's order and aesthetics. However, all you have to do is look around you to conclude that in many Polish cities and towns neither the order nor the aesthetics are not the elements that dominate the surrounding landscape, but, on the contrary, they are very often pushed into an absolute margin. In the view of the applicants, one of the reasons for this is that, in the current legal state, the aesthetics of public space have not been mentioned in the Law on Government Administrations, which has resulted in inconsistencies and ambiguities as to the competence of ministers in this area. In line with the applicant's intention, the proposed project is to remedy this situation and step up actions aimed at ensuring the aesthetics of public space as one of the elements of landscape protection and link them to other elements of the country's cultural policy. To this end, it is proposed, inter alia. adding to the chapter catalogue: culture and the protection of national heritage also issues related to shaping public space aesthetics in architecture, urban planning and spatial planning. Strengthening the efforts of the Council of Ministers will certainly allow for better control in this area, although there is no doubt that administrative control alone will not solve all the problems involved. However, we believe that this is a step in the right direction, which, in conjunction with other actions in the area of widely understood culture, has the chance to improve the current situation. According to the proposed proposal, the amendments also concern the powers of the President of the Council of Ministers, which will enable him to coordinate the State's housing policy and control its organisation. On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I would like to express my support for the proposed changes.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u247	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Extraordinary Committee on Deregulation, I have the honour to present a report on the Government's draft Law of Entrepreneurs, Prints No. 2051 and 2187. The Sejm at its 53rd meeting on 8 December 2017 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Extraordinary Commission on Deregulation for its consideration. The Extraordinary Commission for Deregulation considered the draft law at its meeting on 12 January 2018. During the committee's deliberations, the Legislative Bureau of the Sejm tabled a number of legislative amendments which largely deal with editorial issues in the various articles of the Act. They were largely accepted by the committee. In addition, an amendment to Article was tabled. 5 draft Law of Entrepreneurs, where after paragraph. paragraph 5 is added. 6 as follows: due income as referred to in paragraph. 1, the amounts due, even if not actually received, shall be understood, after excluding the value of the goods returned, of the discounts and the sum granted. This amendment was made in connection with the comments made by traders indicating that the concept of income due to which Article is used. 5 Business rights defining non-registered activities are imprecise. It is proposed to clarify by applying a construction similar to the definition of income due under Article. Article 14 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Act of 26 July 1991 on personal income tax. Another amendment was tabled in Article. Article 66 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the draft law as follows: the results of the evaluations and analyses referred to in paragraph 1. 1 shall be included in the explanatory memorandum to the draft normative act or in the impact assessment of the regulation as a separate part of the explanatory memorandum of the draft normative act, provided that the obligation to draw up such an assessment arises from separate provisions. This is due to the need to ensure consistency with the current legal situation and the possible elimination of interpretation doubts which could arise after the entry into force of the provision. The draft Law of Entrepreneurs is the implementation of the Responsible Development Plan and the Responsible Development Strategy. [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] which will regulate in a holistic and consistent manner the general principles of doing business in Poland and which, by creating favourable, transparent and stable conditions for doing business and strengthening the guarantee of freedom and the rights of entrepreneurs, will reduce business risks and increase the willingness of entrepreneurs to take up and pursue business activities, including technological risks associated with the implementation of innovative projects. The Law on Business Freedom has already been amended 90 times and has become illegible and inconsistent. The right of entrepreneurs is to maintain the primacy over other laws governing economic activity. The most important provisions are the introduction of the principle of "what is not prohibited," the principles of presumption of integrity of the trader, the principles of friendly interpretation of the rules, the principles of proportionality, the principles of information provision, the appointment of a spokesperson for small and medium-sized enterprises. The Act introduces the so-called. start-up package for young companies, the possibility of dealing with some simple official matters by phone, e-mail or other means of communication and gradually eliminates REGON. The company's identification number will be the NIP. A catalogue of forms of regulation of economic activities has been organized. A catalogue of basic rules has also been created to guide the creation of economic law. Ministers and central authorities will issue legal explanations which will be written in simple language to facilitate the application of the most difficult rules. The institution of individual interpretation was also maintained, restrictions were introduced on the control of the exercise of economic activity. It is also an obligation to seek to limit the imposition of administrative obligations and information obligations, as well as the principle of minimum implementation of European Union law and international law, which is understood as meaning that the projector is obliged to avoid over-regulation of the areas covered by the implementing rules beyond what is necessary for their proper implementation. The Law of Entrepreneurs was designed with the assumption that it is not only intended to constitute the main core of the legal framework for business activities, but is also intended to be a reference to the interpretation and application of the provisions of related legal acts. The aim is to simplify and shorten the new legislation governing the rights of entrepreneurs so that it contains only general, key provisions for them. In conclusion, after considering the Government's draft Entrepreneur's Law Act at the Extraordinary Commission on Deregulation on 12 January 2018, the committee asks the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u255	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the pleasure and the honour to submit a report by the Extraordinary Commission on deregulation relating to the government draft Law on Business Law and other laws on business activities in the field of legislation implementing the Law, print No. 2051, 2187. The Council of Ministers adopted a draft Law on the introduction of the Law of Entrepreneurs and other laws on economic activity submitted by the Minister for Development and Finance. The project will ensure implementation into the Polish legal system of solutions provided for in the laws of the business constitution package. The Business Constitution will replace the Freedom of Business Act, which is today a central act of economic law. The pillar of the business constitution is a package of five laws introduced by the government, which is to be the legal basis for the creation and development of Polish entrepreneurship. The Business Law project introduces a catalogue of rules governing business relations with the state, e.g. such as: which is not prohibited by law, the principle of presumption of honesty of the trader and the principle of proportionality is allowed. Another proposal is a runway for new companies, which consists of two solutions: so-called. non-registered activity - activities with small income without the need to register forms, and relief for start-up - half a year without the need to register the company and half a year without social security contributions for beginners. This law is supposed to bring almost PLN 8 billion in savings to companies within 10 years. With the consent of the entrepreneur, some simple official matters can be handled by phone or email. The Authority will thus be able to inform quickly e.g. the need to report annexes or ready to receive documents. The REGON number will be gradually withdrawn from the market. The basic identification number of the entrepreneur will be the NIP. The project also allows for a very flexible suspension of business activities for longer periods than currently foreseen for 24 months or for an indefinite period. The rights of entrepreneurs guaranteed in the business constitution will be protected by the spokesperson of small and medium-sized enterprises. He will be able to, for example. apply to the Minister for clarification of the most complex rules and intervene when the rights of entrepreneurs are violated. The business constitution also includes a proposal for a new law on the principles of participation of foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons in trade in Poland. Until now, these regulations have been inconsistent and scattered in three different laws, and now foreign investors will receive one comprehensive legal act. The implementation of the legal arrangements provided for in the business constitution provides for the draft implementing rules. The draft Law Introducing the Law will replace the current Law of 2 July 2004 on freedom of business with a view to rebuilding and reforming legislation concerning the institutional environment of entrepreneurs and their business activities. The Law of 2 July 2004 on freedom of economic activity has been in force since 2004. As a result of numerous amendments, the changes have become opaque and do not achieve their basic objective: to be a set of principles of economic law. Therefore, it is necessary to introduce solutions that will give a new impetus to the development of Polish entrepreneurship. At the same time, the new solutions proposed in the Business Law Act require changes in many specific laws, so it is proposed to draw up rules implementing the Business Law Act. This project is examined together with the draft Business Law Act, because the introductory provisions are secondary to the provisions of the draft Business Law Act and do not, in principle, contain spontaneous solutions of a substantive nature. Today, this draft law brings together scattered regulations contained in more than 180 laws governing economic space in Poland. I repeat: in 180 laws, not so much regulations and other legal acts, but in laws that regulate economic space in Poland. This draft law essentially brings together all these provisions in a single act. The proper vaccination of all major projects in the Polish legal system is to be carried out by the introductory law. In addition to making a number of amendments to the specific laws relating to the spokesperson of small and medium-sized enterprises, non-registered activities, relief to start, with a change of reference or a transfer of provisions on regulated activities, it provides for it also provides solutions to facilitate businesses' activities, including the possibility for an entrepreneur who is a natural person to provide procura, as well as the introduction into the Code of Administrative Procedure of amendments to the principle of ensuring the quality of the services of the parties to proceedings and the extension of the number of deformed ways of dealing with official matters. The first of the proposed changes was initially processed as exclusive to the Law of Entrepreneurs. Later, however, it was decided to make it universal and to refer to all administrative procedures. The second objective is to improve and speed up the process of handling official matters, so that it will be possible, with the agreement of the party, to handle the matter by electronic means, which I mentioned earlier. The repeal of the Anachronic Act of 1982 on the principles of conducting economic activity in the territory of the Polish People's Republic in the field of small-scale manufacturing by foreign legal and natural persons and an indication of how to adapt the form of conducting business activities by entities acting on the basis of it, changes in the laws referring to the Polish People's Republic and its institutions introducing references to the current legal order and the appropriate name of the State - it also contains provisions of this legal act. Let me conclude by saying that this is a long-awaited set of rules that harmonises economic space with one legal act. I can say with satisfaction that 229 articles have been accepted by the committees with practically no reservations, except for the editorial corrections of the Legislative Bureau. The Commission recommends that the High Sejm adopt the committee's report on the government draft law Rules introducing the Law of Entrepreneurs and other business-related laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u257	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Some things in the speeches will repeat themselves, but I think it is worth it to sound, because this is indeed a very important change that we are making. The business constitution in the package of five laws significantly changes the economic reality in our country, taking part in the Responsible Development Strategy and implementing the constitutional principle of freedom of business. The business constitution will replace the existing law on freedom of business, which is today the central act of economic law, and which, as we have heard, has seen more than 90 amendments since 2004. Work on this package of laws lasted almost 2 years. Pre-consultation and public consultations have identified the main challenges facing the Polish business. Draft laws were also addressed to the opinion of the Social Dialogue Council. Their main assumptions were adopted by the Economic Committee of the Council of Ministers. The drafts were also welcomed by the Joint Committee of the Government and the Territorial Government. The distribution of legislation in five legal acts linked to each other structurally and substantively is intended to give them better and more transparent systems and to organise them properly. This procedure also causes that none of the draft laws is unnecessarily long and excessively complicated, thus fulfilling the request of entrepreneurs regarding the need to maintain the conciseness and factuality of legal acts in the field of Polish economic law. The foundation of the whole package is the Business Law Act. This law contains a catalogue of general principles relating to economic law. These rules, despite their generality, have a real impact on all types of business proceedings, including controls and tax procedures. These principles include the principle: which is not prohibited by law, the principle of the presumption of fairness of the trader, the principle of a friendly interpretation of the rules and the settlement of factual doubts for the benefit of the trader, the principle of legal certainty, i.e. not giving rise without a legitimate reason to the established practice of resolving cases - this principle ensures the predictability of the conduct of the administration and protects them from excessive discretion by the authorities, the principle of proportionality, i.e. not imposing unjustified burdens on the trader. The draft law redefines economic activity, introduces non-registered activity, which responds to doubts arising in administrative practice as to when small commercial activities meet the requirements that qualify it as an economic activity. The project provides a relief for the start. This relief provides that beginners could be exempted from compulsory social security for the first 6 months of their business and would then benefit from the so-called. a small ZUS on general principles. Legal explanations will be introduced and the trader who applies them will not be able to bear the negative consequences. Ministers and bodies submitting draft legal acts to the Council of Ministers will be able to issue legal explanations of their own motion or at the request of the Ombudsman of small and medium-sized enterprises. The primary identifier will be the NIP number and there will be a systematic withdrawal of the REGON number. An entrepreneur who is a natural person will be able to suspend his activity for any period of time specified or indefinite. There will be a systematisation of forms of regulation, with the distinction of concessions, permits and registers of regulated activities. The Act also introduces rules for drafting draft normative acts in the field of economic law. Under the next law, a spokesman for small and medium-sized enterprises will be appointed. He shall be appointed for a single 6-year term by the President of the Council of Ministers on a proposal from the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs, after consulting representative employers' organisations. Within its remit, the Ombudsman will have the right: to apply for a legislative initiative; to apply for the so-called. Legal explanations; make an assessment of the functioning of legal acts relating to economic life; ask the Supreme Court to resolve a discrepancy in the interpretation of the law; ask the Supreme Administrative Court to clarify the legal provisions; request administrative proceedings, bring actions before the administrative court, participate in prosecutions on the rights of the prosecutor; lodge an emergency complaint with the Supreme Court; request the initiation of pre-trial proceedings in cases of criminal offences initiated ex officio; inform the supervisory authorities or audit authorities of irregularities; mediate between traders and public administrations; submit assessments and proposals to ensure effective protection of the rights of traders and to improve the procedure for dealing with cases in this respect. The next act is the Act on Central Registration and Information on Economic Activity and Information Point for the Entrepreneur. CEIDG will include business records and business information. The publication by the trader in CEIDG of information on the proxy will be equivalent to the granting of a power of attorney in writing. It will be possible to automatically resume business activity after the suspension period. The relevant data will be made available without burdening the trader. New communication channels will be created with entrepreneurs. The Entrepreneur's Information Point will be a source of complete information and must not contain references to other provisions. It shall contain information on the rules and conditions for carrying out inspections and inspections with traders. The point will allow to obtain certificates of non-payment of social security contributions, health insurance, the Labour Fund and the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund and of non-payment with tax obligations and will allow the payment of a tax fee. Another project is a law on the rules of participation of foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons in trade in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The project collects, systematizes and corrects the provisions contained so far in two different laws, i.e. the Business Freedom Act and the Services Act of 2010. It shall establish the entry of a representative for a fixed period, i.e. for a period of 2 years, which will ensure that the alert is kept up to date. It allows for the waiver of information about the person authorised in the branch to represent it, as well as the obligation to attach a notarially certified signature pattern. When registering, he shall exempt from the obligation to submit his entitlement to use the premises for the purpose of the headquarters of the representative office. The decision to prohibit the conduct of business shall be replaced by the deletion from the register of delegations. And the last project - Provisions introducing the Law of Entrepreneurs and other laws concerning business activity. These provisions reflect the amendments made to more than 180 laws. Among other things, they enable a businessman who is a natural person to provide procura. They improve the quality of service of the parties to proceedings, increase the number of deformed ways of dealing with cases, enable the handling of cases by electronic means. They repeal the provisions of the 1982 Act on the principles of conducting economic activity in the territory of the Polish People's Republic in the field of small manufacturing by foreign entities. They amend the laws referring to the Polish People's Republic and its institutions, introducing references to the current legal order and the proper name of the Polish State. It should also be pointed out that all the laws were assessed by the Committee on the Economy, those opinions are opinions that speak of not submitting comments, they are positive opinions, even though there were various proposals for possible corrections, which were the subject of an in-depth discussion. The Law and Justice Club positively, I could say, and even very positively opinion the package of laws named: the Business Constitution. Laws meet the need to merge in these five legal acts legislation on the broad economic sphere. There is a reflection on the extent to which the proposed regulations are complementary to the new state policy concerning Polish economic operators and foreign entities. Let us remind you that the policies implemented by previous governments were indeed the implementation of the doctrine of economic dependence, namely that developed countries They get rich at the expense of poorer countries. This led to a dichotomy between low-income outermost countries and high-income countries despite GDP growth in nominally developing countries. As a result, the difference in economic structure between developed countries and developing countries has continued to grow. Professor Thomas Piketty, one of the most famous and valued economists in the world, says today: Poland is an economically colonized country. The current government is going to reverse this phenomenon. He sees an opportunity for Poland to reindustrialise industry and strengthen those sectors that can exploit relative competitive advantages. In this way, growth and convergence can be accelerated by using economic niches in an open, globalised economic world. This is why the Free and Solidarnia Circle supports the government's strategy for responsible development, which uses the structural economy instrumentarium. Industrial diversification, industrial modernisation consists primarily of gathering knowledge and know-how in new industrial sectors, coordinating industrially related investments, compensating for external information costs for pioneers. The integrated management of special economic zones brings good results, and foreign direct investment is being placed in increasingly technologically advanced projects. In addition, it should be noted that the proposed laws aim to improve the legal environment and reduce the risk of entrepreneurs and transaction costs for individual companies. The proposals contained in the proposed laws will stimulate the economic development process based on the accumulation of human and physical capital, thus launching a causal chain leading to industry 4.0, whose components are innovation and productivity, efficient management, advanced technologies and ethical infrastructure, legal infrastructure. The Business Constitution sets new standards for the creation and functioning of the Polish economic system. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u323	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, I have the honour to present a report on the government draft law amending the National Court Register and certain other laws. The Commission on Justice and Human Rights considered the project on 9 and 24 January 2018. I will briefly point out that the law concerns the implementation of European laws, the synchronization of the National Court Register with such registers operating in Europe, but also on the occasion of the amendment makes great progress in the computerization and improvement of the processes that the National Court Register has so far been engaged in. The register in many elements will be an electronic register, it will be a register which, apart from a few exceptions, will operate, at least, the circulation of documents in electronic form between the parties. This will result in a significant acceleration of proceedings, which in an era of rapid increase in entries and responsibilities of the courts in this area is indisputable and absolutely necessary. Of course, there are a number of procedures and tools for courts, which result from many years of practice, which show that such solutions are necessary, for example, to specify the coercive procedure, namely the procedure which requires the obliged entity to complete the registration formalities in connection with the expiry of the statutory deadlines. So far, there have been a lot of doubts, problems and ambiguities as to how much the court could afford to call on those obliged to perform these duties, how much it could correspond with the natural persons who represented the authorities, except for those authorities, in so far as it could obtain addresses, such as the addresses of the persons who represented the companies. Today all this in this law will be regulated, the registry court will have very hard tools to enforce the registration obligation. This will provide certainty, guaranteeing that the register is a truly reliable source of information and a calendar of activities most often of limited liability companies. The Commission has introduced some minor amendments from a substantive point of view. First of all, it minimally postponed the introduction of a repository of notarial acts, which form the basis for entries in the National Court Register. This is an extraordinary simplification of the procedure, where notarial acts underlying the alert will be in the electronic repository, but it takes time for it to be introduced. Of course, the notary government also called for a slight delay in the entry into force of this repository. Therefore, the deadline for moving the implementation of the law was set for 15 March, which slightly delayed the entry into force of the law and slightly delayed the entry into force of this repository. The Commission, of course, calls for the adoption of a draft law in accordance with the text annexed to the report and for further examination of the draft. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Associations, foundations and public health establishments will have a choice as to whether to use electronic form. The aim of this solution is to improve the functioning of the National Court Register. E-mails will also be sent to remedy the deficiencies, letters submitted and remedies. Since 1 March 2020, registration files are to be kept in electronic form, but financial documents will be submitted in electronic form before that date. Entrepreneurs will be required to submit financial statements electronically. The financial statements will be forwarded by the National Court Register to the Ministry of Finance. The application for the entry of persons representing the trader and the winding-up and procurators will need to be accompanied, in addition to the declaration of appointment, by addresses. Final decisions shall be issued and served in electronic form. It will not be necessary to provide a justification for a provision in matters other than the alert if the applicant and the participants have taken part in the proceedings. If an application is submitted in electronic form, further letters will also have to be submitted in electronic form. Ladies and gentlemen! The project envisages the creation of a Central Repository for Electronic Releases of Notarial Acts. According to the project, the institution of the registry curator shall be liquidated. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u333	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government's draft law amending the Act on the National Court Register and certain other laws. The National Court Register has been in operation since 1 January 2001 under the Law of 20 August 1997. It was created in place of the Commercial Register, whose history dates back to the period of the Second Republic of Poland. During the period of its existence, the National Court gave citizens the opportunity to obtain information about entities subject to an alert, thereby strengthening the certainty and safety of economic turnover and the confidence of citizens in the institutions of the State. The importance of the register shows that, according to data at the end of 2015, the list of entities registered in it exceeded 400 000. The continuous development of the KRS, for example in the breakthrough of June 2012, since it is possible to download up-to-date electronic copies of entries of entities free of charge via the Internet, does not change the fact that it is necessary to introduce changes that will adapt the register to dynamically developing economic turnover, enhancing its security, as well as facilitating and improving the registration procedure through registered courts. Mr Michał Woś, undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Justice, rightly stressed that the proposed changes aim to bridge the narrow throat of the KRS in economic trade. It is time to fully adapt the KRS to the 21st century so that all registration procedures can only be carried out through the electronic system, without the need to produce a large number of documents in paper form. It is not exaggerated that the proposed changes are revolutionary in the positive sense of the word. The project provides for the amendment of the Act on the National Courts of Justice and other laws, e.g. Code of Civil Procedure and Commercial Companies Code. The proposed amendments can be grouped, in accordance with the terminology used in the justification of the project, into four categories: firstly, changes strengthening the certainty and safety of trading, such as coercive proceedings, strengthening the powers of a curator acting under the provisions of the Civil Code, secondly, changes relating to the use of new information technologies and the need to implement Directive (EU) 2017/1132 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 June 2017 on certain aspects of company law in the field of the integration of central, commercial and company registers, thirdly, changes involving a number of activities for entrepreneurs, such as simplification of the proceedings on the so-called '. registration entries in heading 4 of the KRS or disclosure in the Central Register of Restructuring and Failures of information on the arrears of entities, and finally fourthly, a number of minor but extremely important changes of a procedural and order nature, such as the harmonisation of terminology contained in k.p.c. in Section VI: Registration proceedings, in the KRS Act and in the registry pledge and pledge register law, and a change concerning the introduction of the definition of a statutory electronic system. High Izbo! This is a very good bill, which perfectly complements the previously discussed bill of the Law of Entrepreneurs and is part of the implementation of the Strategy for Responsible Development, which is why it fully deserves to be adopted by the High Sejm. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle clearly welcomes this bill and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much.

In theory, we could submit some forms of application online, and on the other hand they were printed, sealed and the whole procedure was preserved. The point is that entrepreneurs can choose whether they want to submit applications in paper form or in electronic form, as is the case with associations or foundations. I want to say here that we are very intensively analyzing, we are considering going the other way, i.e. encouraging associations and foundations to be obliged, including them, to use the ICT system, of course with the full support infrastructure of entities that would not necessarily be sufficiently qualified to make such a request over the Internet. As for the specific questions, Mr Meysztowicz asked for the deadline. Mr President, there is an instructional period of 7 days already set in the KRS Act. EU law provides for 30 days here, we have this 7-day instructional term. As we know, in many cases it is not preserved, but the aim of this ICT system is to make it not even 7 days, but to make it even faster, as soon as possible, so that the entire procedure is conducted and appropriate entries are carried out within the KRS with immediate effect. Mr Suchoń asked about the computerisation and implementation of the IT system. I want to tell you that when we first consider the issue, for example. those systems that have already been created, i.e. the register of perpetrators of sexual crimes or the drawing of cases, the initial valuation of the market of these two systems was many times higher than the costs we incurred when building both systems in the context of the comprehensive computerization of the state, but this is about the piece for which we are responsible, that is, the justice system. So we want to preserve all the property rights to these systems and we will most likely decide on such a path that we will build it with our own forces of ministry, of course with the support of various modern forms, such as the body of leasing IT specialists, specialists and experts. One last question. Mr Wojciechowski asked about obtaining information from the KRS. Mr President, the National Court of Auditors provides for two possibilities for wide-ranging electronic viewing of these documents, namely, on the one hand, it will be a fully public repository of documents, financial statements, a repository of financial statements and the other, also available, a part of which will be an electronic file that can be viewed from board to board, sitting in front of your computer at home, not as it is now when you have to order the file well in advance and of course these files are to be reviewed, but on the spot, in court. In this respect, full disclosure will be maintained in this bill, and that is what we wanted. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u343	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government's draft law amending the Act on the National Court Register and certain other laws, prints No 2067 and 2203. The aim of the project is to implement Directive (EU) 2017/1132 of the European Parliament and of the Council on certain aspects of company law to the extent that the Directive provides for the establishment of a system of interconnection of registers requiring the exchange of information and registration documents in electronic form. The outdated IT system of the National Court Register requires that the transfer of solutions proposed in EU law requires a number of legal and technical solutions, such as: - speed up and streamline the registration procedure and facilitate the party's contact with the court through the obligation to submit an application for entry in the register of KRS entrepreneurs using the electronic system, electronic contact with the registry court, the introduction of registration files in electronic form, the creation of an electronic repository of acts and notarial protocols, and the integration of the KRS into the BRIS system, - to provide registration courts with the opportunity to discipline those obliged to appoint representatives of the entity when they refrain from carrying out their duties, - the liquidation of the institution of the probation officer, - the creation of a publicly accessible Internet portal enabling parties and other economic operators to have access to all documents submitted to the National Courts of Auditors, - the burden on registry courts of introducing an obligation to submit financial statements in electronic form only and many other necessary regulations. The discussion at first reading and during committee work has shown that the proposed amendment is necessary. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the Government's submission.

It is new in the sense that it will be a completely new law, both externally and internally, where the Marshals' Guard and Parliament will carry out their tasks. Due to traditions, experience and the current risks of security solutions, including the protection of other national parliaments, there are very different risks. However, it must be stressed that one thing that brings them together is that in all of them it is the President of Parliament - in our case it would be the Marshal of the Sejm - that they have the main competences in this area, and that the activities of strict security are carried out by those who are professionally prepared and who have the status of an officer assigned to the service of that State.
