In the course of the committee's work, provisions concerning the protection of Members and Senators outside the Sejm's premises and facilities were waived in order to obtain consensus. Similarly, in order to obtain consensus, provisions concerning the use by the Marshal's Guard of the help of persons not officers of the Marshal's Guard were waived. At first, we wondered whether such persons who recognized whether they were doing an interview could work outside the Sejm, but it was abandoned. The provisions of the Law on Solutions allowing police officers, State Security Services and other services to move to the Marshal's Guard were supplemented. We have also clarified and clarified the provisions concerning the tasks of the Marshal's Guard and the rights and duties of Marshal's Guard officers, including in correlation with the solutions set out in the Law on State Protection in this respect. Rules on disciplinary action against Marshals' Guard officers have also been clarified. The most important changes to the current legal situation concern the extension of the competence of the Marshal's Guard. At the same time, she is granted new tasks, including: to carry out the pyrotechnical and radiological diagnosis of persons, vehicles, areas and objects of the Sejm and the Senate, and to take action to neutralise these threats and detect listening devices in areas, facilities and devices on the Board of the Sejm Chancellery and the Board of the Senate Chancellery. Secondly, an officer of the Marshal's Guard will have the right on the territory of the Sejm and the Senate, among others, to carry out tasks. issuing orders, legiting, stopping, making checks. The way in which these actions are carried out will, of course, be entitled to a complaint against the prosecutor responsible for the place of the action. The provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure relating to appeal proceedings will apply to the complaint. Third, the Marshal's Guard officer will be entitled to use or use direct coercion measures and to use or use firearms under the rules laid down in the Act of 24 May 2013 on direct coercion and firearms. It should be added that under the current rules, Marshals' Guard staff also have such powers. The weapon is used to carry out protective tasks of the Sejm and the Senate and to secure convoys of classified documents and monetary values. The Marshal's Guard will not have the authority to conduct operational or investigative activities. The Marshal's Guard officers released from service will be entitled to pension supplies for service of years or in the event of total incapacity for service, and members of their families - in the event of death of the host, under the terms and conditions laid down in the 1994 Act on pension provision of police officers, ABW, Intelligence Agency, Military Counterintelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service, CBA, Border Guard, Government Protection Office, State Fire and Prison Service and their families. High Seym! The solutions contained in the draft law concerning the applicable degrees in the Marshal's Guard should be accompanied by the provisions of the draft law, among others. rules allowing the undisciplined transition of officers from other services to the Marshal's Guard. We want the door to the service in the Marshal's Guard to be open, especially to those who are prepared or who consider the service in uniform to be a kind of challenge, mission, professional purpose. As regards remuneration, it should be noted that the bill does not provide for a number of allowances in the salaries of officers of the Marshal's Guard who are currently receiving other services. Expenditure related to the security of the Sejm and the Senate, i.e. the maintenance of the Marshal's Guard, is currently planned and implemented from the part of the budget at the disposal of the Sejm Chancellery. Such a solution is also envisaged after the entry into force of the Marshal's Guard Act. The Marshal's Guard will continue to function in the structures of the Sejm Chancellery and, above all, will be subject to the Marshal of the Sejm. In addition, a number of tasks will be provided by other organizational cells of the Sejm Chancellery. The services under the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration are financed by the Ministry's budget, but e.g. Military police or Customs are no longer financed by the Ministry's funds. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u382	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report of the Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary and Immunity Committee on the draft resolution on amending the Rules of Procedure of the Polish Sejm, No. 2009, and a draft resolution on amending the Rules of Procedure of the Polish Sejm presented by the Sejm Bureau, No. 2112. Marshal of the Sejm, based on art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 16 November 2017, the draft resolution in print No 2009 and on 8 December 2017 submitted by the Sejm Bureau a draft resolution in print No 2112 to the Parliament's Rules of Procedure Committee for First Reading. The Commission carried out the first reading of the draft resolution in print no. 2009 at its meeting on 21 November 2017 and the first reading of the draft resolution in print no. 2112 on 14 December 2017. The draft amendment to the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm provides for the introduction of legal solutions which are designed by the Marshal of the Sejm to ensure the seriousness and order of the sitting room. In particular, the proposed provisions aim to reduce non-regulatory Members' speeches. A new ordering authority has been provided for the Marshal of the Sejm. If he finds that he has violated the seriousness of the Sejm by his conduct in the sitting room, he will be able to reprimand him. On this basis, the Bureau of the Sejm will be able to adopt a resolution to reduce the Member's salary to an amount not exceeding half of that salary or diet in full for a maximum period of 3 months. The draft also provides for the question to be asked before the vote to be decided by the Marshal of the Sejm. If Marshal Sejm allows questions to be asked before the vote, only one Member from the club or the circle will be able to ask the question. These amendments aim to counter the abuse of formal proposals and corrections by Members of the institutions. It is therefore proposed to introduce the power of the Marshal of the Sejm to request formal applications in writing, as well as to introduce a mechanism for not considering identical formal applications. The draft also provides for a correction time limit of 1 minute. The proposed regulation also concerns time limits for meetings of committees, sub-committees and parliamentary teams. It introduces a 3-week cycle, not, as it currently does, a 2-week cycle. The proposed regulation introduces the principle that the meetings of the Sejm committees are held on days when the Sejm does not take place, while leaving the Marshal the possibility of not agreeing to the sitting of the committee. If there is a need for a committee meeting on the day on which the Sejm is held, such a meeting may be held only if the Marshal of the Sejm agrees. The consequence of these arrangements is that the committee which was convened with the agreement of the Marshal on the day of the Sejm's deliberations cannot extend its agenda. These solutions are also applicable to subcommittees and parliamentary teams. Already during the committee meeting, many Members pointed out that the introduction of a three-week series of Sejm meetings would lead to the fact that Members would not have enough time to meet their constituents in their districts. This would certainly also increase travel costs for those Members who are not from Warsaw. Five amendments were tabled during the committee's work. They concerned the possibility of holding committee meetings and meetings of the Sejm after consideration of the agenda, the deletion of the entry concerning the Marshal's consent to the sitting of the committee, when it is to be held on the date on which the Sejm is not held, and the need to consider the current information submitted by the opposition clubs at least one meeting per month. The Commission rejected four amendments and, as suggested by the Legislative Office, one on the dates of entry into force of the 2009 and 2012 amendments to the Rules of Procedure, adopted. The Commission shall, in accordance with Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm shall, at the request of the applicant, submit a request for minority information which concerns current information, in particular the number of questions raised by individual parliamentary clubs and the time limits for examining them. Following the examination of both projects at its meeting on 14 December 2017, the Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary and Immunity Affairs Committee asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft resolution. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The draft amends the provisions of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm concerning the selection and appointment and dismissal of public bodies which are linked to the entry into force of the Act of 8 December 2017 amending the Act on the National Council of Courts and certain other laws. The resolution is the consequence of the entry into force of the Act on the National Judicial Council. The draft resolution provides for the introduction of a new provision in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm - Article. 29a paragraph. 1 extending the catalogue of actions undertaken by the Sejm for the selection of members of the National Judicial Council from the judges of the Supreme Court, universal courts, administrative courts and military courts, in accordance with the rules and procedures laid down in the Act on the National Judicial Council, and grants the Commission of Justice and Human Rights in paragraph 1. 2 of this Article powers to determine the list of candidates on which the Sejm will vote in accordance with the delegation set out in Article. 11d paragraph. The 4th KRS Act. According to Art. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland The National Judicial Council is the guardian of the independence of the courts and independence of judges. In order to achieve this objective, it is necessary to balance and control each other between the three authorities: legislative, executive and judicial. None of these authorities can be independent of the other, it cannot be, so to speak, insensitive to the fact that these other authorities are somehow interacting with each other, because these three authorities should serve the public, should serve the citizens, should serve the Polish people. According to the KRS Act, the Sejm elects 15 members of the Council from among the judges of the Supreme Court, general courts, administrative courts and military courts for a four-year term. It is also worth recalling that the argument of submission to the National Court Register to politicians is false, it is completely wrong, because it is not politicians, but the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, which is a constitutional body of the state, will elect members of the National Council of the Court. In the light of the provision of the Article. 187 of the Constitution, which states that the Act sets out the rules and procedures for the election of the members of the National Judicial Council, this project is compatible with the Constitution. The spirit of the rights in which the principle of three divisions of power is laid down. Why am I talking about this? Therefore, in order to make the High House aware that it has been 360 years between the moment when this text was published and today. Look at this from the perspective of this principle constantly invoked by our opponents, namely the principles of the threefold division of power by Charles Montescius. If we were to say today what model this principle of three divisions of power was referring to, then probably most of the parliamentarians would say that this is actually impossible. I would therefore like to remind you that the executive authority is the power exercised by the king and by his ministers. The legislative authority is the Chamber of Commons, the House of Lords. As far as judicial authority is concerned, it was a completely different model than that used in continental Europe, and it was always different from that of the European model. How, then, can we apply this principle directly today? Yeah, you can. One can refer to the simple principle, which is contained in this model, the principle of three divisions of power, and first of all, that each of these three fundamental entities, i.e. the executive authority, the legislative authority and the judicial authority, in accordance with the principle of Montescius is created in a different way, and secondly that their separateness exists, so there is their independence and, most importantly, through mutual inhibition, the restraint takes place between the forces of these three authorities. The Act on the National Judicial Council in its essence aimed at restoring the proper measure, restoring the right balance between the legislative authority, the executive authority and the judiciary. The point was that during these twenty few years of the existence of the Third Republic, the judicial authority gained a strong advantage over the other two authorities. If we add that the model of Charles Montescius did not provide for decisions relating to the functioning of the tribunal, then we will understand that this principle is a very general principle, firstly, and secondly, that principle of the threefold division of power, in order to understand and apply it properly, should be accepted as such a principle which is transformed, is changed, because it was created in 1748, then it was first described. Thus, when we are discussing a change in the Rules of Procedure today, first of all, this change in the Rules of Procedure will in practice restore the balance between the three authorities and thus restore the Montesque principle of balance between the authorities, and secondly, create a mechanism or contribute to the fact that this judicial system will actually be a viable system and not a system that protects the interests of only corporations of judges. We proceed with the consideration of item 16 of the agenda: Report of the Committee on Culture and Media on the Commission's draft resolution on the celebration of the 100th anniversary of the Polish Geographic Society (prints no. 2169 and 2175). I ask Mrs Małgorzata Chmiel to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u540	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I would like to present a resolution on the celebration of the 100th anniversary of the Polish Geographic Society. On January 27, 1918, the Polish Geographic Society was founded in Warsaw as one of the first scientific organizations to be formed together with the rebirth of the Republic of Poland. Its founders were outstanding scholars, teachers, socialists and patriots. The organization of the Polish Geographic Society since 1918 was the Geographic Review, which is certainly widely known to you, and which since 1953. - and presently - is published by the Institute of Geography and Spatial Management of the Polish Academy of Sciences in Warsaw. The Society played an extremely important role in the territorial integration of Poland after the period of partitions. The development of geographers, especially Prof. Eugeniusz Romer, made it much easier to determine the borders of reborn Poland during the Paris Conference. I wanted to point out at this point that there were no maps or documents at that time that would allow these boundaries to be developed. It was thanks to our geographers that we managed to inventory all of this. After regaining independence, geographers began working on the effective development of the country and started a broad educational, patriotic and educational action to raise national and social awareness. The Society also operated in the years of World War II, although, of course, to a much smaller extent, and many of its members were killed by German and Soviet invaders. Then it actively joined the process of rebuilding the destroyed Poland, including the development of the so-called. Land of the Recovered. The geographers participated in the development of all concepts of land-use planning and changes in administrative division, in the diagnosis, forecasting of natural and socio-economic changes. Geographical Olympics for secondary school students have played an important educational role since 1974, which have so far been very successful. The measure of appreciation for Polish geography in the international arena was represented among others. the election of Prof. Stanisław Leszczycki as President of the International Geographical Union, which was held in 1968 and 1972. On the 100th anniversary of the establishment of the Polish Geographical Society the Polish Sejm expresses appreciation and respect for this scientific institution and the entire environment of Polish geographers. The great contribution of the society and its members to the construction of independent Poland, the knowledge and definition of the territory of the Polish state, the development of Polish science, as well as the process of shaping national identity, civic upbringing and the patriotic attitude of society, including many generations of young people, deserve special emphasis. 100 years ago, the geographical society was working well, today too. Now he also publishes magazines in English version. On behalf of the committee, I ask all of you to support this resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

As a result of this commitment to defend Polish independence, many Polish geographers were persecuted by German and Soviet invaders and many even murdered. In the year when we worship the century of regaining independence, it is especially worth recalling not only this scientific but also independent activity of one of the most outstanding Polish geographers Prof. Eugeniusz Romer, among others. Vice President of the International Geographic Union. He was the son of Edmund, the insurgent of January, and the brother of John, General of the Polish Army. It's the late '80s. In the 19th century, during the middle school period, he worked in the secret independence movement in Małopolska. In the years preceding World War I he was one of the leaders of the secret Youth Organization of Zarzewie or perhaps the patrons of this youth organization. For these young independence activists he gave a series of lectures, lectures, strengthening national awareness and awareness of the need to fight for independent Poland. He also became one of the leaders of the national and independence movement in recent years before World War I, from which the Polish Military Union, later the Secret Army of Poland and finally the Polish Shooting Teams emerged. And it was Eugeniusz Romer who was president of the Supreme Council of the Polish Shooting Teams in 1911 and 1913. However, it should be remembered that Polish Shooting Teams were the only Polish Legions of Józef Piłsudski who emerged from the Union of Action and Shooter. So that due to both the great scientific achievements of Polish geographers and their commitment to independence activities, as well as due to people such as Prof. Eugeniusz Romer, the Law and Justice Club fully supports the draft resolution. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u544	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The Polish Geographic Society was established on 27 January 1918, i.e. months before the final and official declaration of independence of the Polish state. The first membership declaration was signed by 47 people, among them geographers, economists, historians, sociologists, botanists, anthropologists. The principle of setting up the company was verified immediately, as it is among others. thanks to the development of the members of the company, the boundaries of the new Poland were defined. The basis for their determination during the Paris conference was the statistical-geographical Atlas prepared by Prof. Eugeniusz Romer, published in 1916. In the interwar period, in non-election Poland, the company, based on the diversity of professions of its members, worked for patriotic and educational education, aiming to raise the awareness of the national and social population, which over the last 150 years had limited contact with Polish language and culture. The Society carried out educational activities that also integrate in the sphere of national membership. It also led to the harmonisation of the geographical nomenclature so different after the partition period. The Polish Geographic Society has done a great deal of work to bring tangible results in the sphere of the economy of the newly created country. It examined the different regions in terms of physical-geographical and socio-economic characteristics. The results of their research were later used for economic decisions. From our contemporary point of view, these actions cannot be denied the amount of romance and some kind of precursor. Citizens' and Socialist Tradition The company was aware of itself during difficult years of Polishization of Pomerania, fighting for the rights of Poles in Gdańsk and Silesia. The civilist and patriotic imperative survived among the members of the society also during the Second World War, when among others. from the first days of the war they were exposed to persecution in organized actions to destroy Polish intelligence. The Society worked in the underground, and many of its members: teachers and academics, learned on secret sets, including concentration camps, prisoners' camps and lagras. The Polish Geographic Society joined the underground in a more formal way, among others. through polemic studies published in underground publications of the National Army. It also contributed to the work preparing the Polish position on changes in state borders after the end of the war. In post-war Poland the geographical society took on many tasks, among others. The development of the concept of state spatial development took part in the work to create a new administrative division of the country. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Ladies and gentlemen, 2018 is the year of the 100th anniversary of the founding of the company. Many scientific institutions participated in the anniversary celebration, and the company prepared a series of jubilee conferences on this occasion. This draft resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the celebration of the 100th anniversary of the Polish Geographic Society aims to express appreciation and respect for the achievements of the Polish Geographic Society and the entire geographical environment, with particular emphasis on the contribution of the company and all its members...

ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u562	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Draft law adopted by the Council of Ministers on 28 December Ub. It is part of the National Housing Programme, which was adopted in 2016. This is print 2192 - a draft law amending the law, which your marshal has already stated in detail, so I will not repeat it. The changes contained in the project concern the rules for assisting local authorities in the creation of housing stock for rent and the rules for the management of the housing stock of the municipality. Within the scope of the Financial Support Act, it is planned to make available to municipal authorities for part of the cost of building apartments for rent, including municipal and protected housing, and for the creation of so-called. intervention stock - these are heating houses, shelters, accommodation units, temporary premises - total amounts of more than PLN 6 billion. This amount corresponds to the identified housing deficits for rent. It is planned that there will be about 170 thousand. new municipal housing. The financial support for municipalities is between 30 and 55% of the costs of the project. An important solution is to increase, from 30 to 50% of the costs of the project, the financing of the purchase of former works flats with the possibility of later selling them to the tenants with a bonus. Here we exclude the withdrawal period, which is valid for this type of co-financing. In that case, he won't be there. It is also important to allow for simultaneous financing of the same projects from the European Union and support under the revised provisions of the Act. In addition, a new financial housing support facility was made available to the municipalities - a non-refundable grant from the Fund of 20% of the costs of the project, whereby an investor other than the municipality will build housing for people who are not eligible for the municipal premises from the housing stock of the municipality, but still need support to meet their housing needs due to the level of their income. There, the rent reduction is up to 4% of the replacement rate per year. The law on the protection of tenants' rights was introduced, among others. the possibility for the municipality to verify the income of the tenants of the housing stock and the corresponding rent increase. This will apply to a situation where the tenant achieves income exceeding the income thresholds set by the municipality. This principle applies only to new contracts concluded after the entry into force of the amended law. It was also possible to terminate the lease contract when the tenant has a legal title to another premises not only in the same but also in a nearby village. Here is an instrument for self-government to determine the issue of this nearby village, because there are different geographical conditions, population conditions, etc. In various areas of Poland this is different, so local governments have their own competence in this area. For persons applying for a tenant from a municipality, a declaration on the amount of household income, a statement on the assets and, at the request of the municipality, a declaration on the absence of a legal title to another establishment located in the same or nearby village under criminal liability is required. The social rent of the apartments has been introduced. The municipality will be able to rent or sublet any premises that comply with the statutory requirements, which should significantly improve the situation or reduce the scale of problems related to the shortage of such premises. So the commune... So we eliminate the so-called. Social resources, we're just building a community resource. The municipality will also be provided with an instrument allowing tenants who occupy very large premises to convert these premises into smaller, more appropriate, with lower charges. And this is also the responsibility of the municipality, i.e. Councils of the municipality. The project eliminates the eviction on the pavement. Persons who have a temporary space may not be evicted to the accommodation unit, but to the temporary room provided by the municipality. Here, too, we introduce new instruments so that such temporary rooms can be adapted or built. The municipalities are also given the opportunity to renovate old municipal resources. So far, funding has only been available for new premises. Such premises, which were eliminated from circulation, which means they could not be inhabited because of the technical condition... At the moment, the municipality will be provided with funds so that it can also renovate such premises and make them available as social premises. We would like the bill to come in on 1 March. Probably a slight shift will be linked to the legislative process. However, I hope that the High House will quickly and efficiently carry out this project throughout the legislative process and that basic provisions will be implemented as soon as possible, i.e. on 1 March. On the other hand, some of the rules will come in late so that the municipalities can prepare properly, and the tenants of the apartments, and those of the existing ones, and those of the new ones that will be occupied, can learn the new rules. I think that these are long-awaited by local authorities, but also by TBS solutions that will allow for a decisive increase in the number of apartments for social rentals. Thank you very much. The economic aspect - I see here a lack of confidence in entrepreneurs or economic activity - is greater openness to what is public. Is that the right direction? I don't quite agree with that. It would be good to make it easier for private parties to cooperate in this law, and here I see such a kind of lack of confidence in private entities, which is revealed in the provisions of this law. It is worth correcting the provisions in order to facilitate their participation in investment activities. It is modern in favour of bringing this draft law to committee, but it seems to me that this project should be dealt with by at least three committees, namely undoubtedly the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, the Committee on Infrastructure, but also the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. There is also no doubt about the creation of a subcommittee. I would just add, last but not least, that it would be good if this law were worked out without haste, with a view to constructively building solutions that would help. Thank you very much. In such a situation, the state must take appropriate steps so that Poles can live under decent conditions. The proposed project introduces a number of changes in the legal solutions for social housing and defines many elements that are part of it. ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u586	High Seym! Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party, I will present a position regarding the government draft law on amending the law on financial support for the creation of social premises, shelters and houses for homeless people, the law on the protection of the rights of tenants, the housing stock of the commune and on the amendment of the Civil Code and some other laws, print no. 2192. Changes in the rules for assisting local authorities in the creation of housing stock for rent. The project proposes to link the deadline for the transfer of budgetary resources - using the form of the special reserve - with the deadlines for the payment of financial support declared by investors. This will provide a realistic response to the investment needs of municipalities for municipal construction. This is the possibility of financing housing investments at a level that covers the estimated deficit of municipal premises. According to the project, in connection with the liquidation of a separate functional part of the housing stock of the municipality, which are social premises, municipalities and single-member municipal companies, which the municipality entrusted with carrying out its own task of meeting the housing needs of the local government community, they will be able to apply for financial support for the construction of municipal premises. This will be possible without the existing requirement to separate the relevant social housing stock from the housing stock of the municipality. The project also clarifies the possibility of applying for financial support for the implementation of a project consisting in the renovation or reconstruction of a residential building which, due to its poor technical condition, has been excluded from use or whose use has been prohibited. The municipality or a single-member municipality was also allowed to buy a share in the residential building. It is proposed to provide support for the creation of temporary premises, which are intended to be rented in case of execution of the eviction judgment from the premises. This will apply to situations where the debtor has not been granted the right to a social lease by the court. Financial support will also be provided for renovation or reconstruction of existing accommodation facilities and shelters for homeless people and projects which will result in heating. An important solution is to allow for simultaneous financing of the same projects from the European Union and to support them on the basis of the revised provisions of the Act. The investor will be able to benefit from this option provided that the support does not cover the same costs of the project which are already financed by EU funds. A new non-refundable financial support instrument was proposed for the creation of housing for rent for lower-income earners, i.e. those who obtain too low income to meet housing needs in commercial conditions and in the rental system of apartments offered on market terms - including the option to pursue property - and too high to apply for a municipal place. It is about the non-refundable financial support provided to municipalities and inter-communal unions to cover part of the costs of the project consisting of the participation of the municipality and the inter-communal relationship in the cost of building premises by another investor. For persons applying for a tenant from a housing estate, the municipality was required to submit a declaration of the amount of household income, a statement of property status and, at the request of the municipality, a declaration of non-legal title to another premises located in the same or nearby village under criminal liability. This means that the establishment of the lease will depend not only on the income of the person concerned but also on his/her property. This will apply to new leases. The social rent of the apartments has been introduced. This means abandoning the concept of social housing and abandoning the need to separate these premises from the housing stock of the municipality. The use of social leases will be based on the fact that the municipality will be able to rent or rent any place that meets the statutory requirements as part of a social lease, which should reduce the scale of problems related to the shortage of social premises.

U. poz. 2486, which implemented into the Polish legal system Directive (EU) 2016/97 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 January 2016 on insurance distribution, hereinafter referred to as Directive 2016/97. In the European Union, a proposal from the European Commission to postpone the date of application of this Directive from 1 October 2018 is being examined. Directive 2016/97 currently sets a deadline for transposition and application as at 23 February 2018. In addition, a group of 16 Member States asked the European Commission to postpone the transposition period and the entry into force of Directive 2066/97 until at least 1 October 2018. Both the European Parliament and a group of Member States justified their proposals by the need to provide the insurance industry with more time needed to better prepare for the implementation and effective application of Directive 206/97 and the delegated Regulations. The European Commission has agreed to take into account the European Parliament's proposal and partly the group of Member States to postpone to 1 October 2018 the deadline from which Member States are to apply the national provisions necessary to comply with Directivepl. As a result of extensive arrangements and public consultations, no objections were raised to the draft position of the government. In view of the above, the proposed law provides for the amendment of the date of entry into force of the Insurance Distribution Act from 23 February 2018 to 1 October 2018. I am pleased to present a report by the Legislative Commission on the adoption of the Senate on the Act amending the Law on the Specific Rules for the Removal of Legal Effects of Reprivatization Decisions on Warsaw Real Estates, issued in violation of the law and certain other laws, contained in print No 2219. The amendment of the Law on the special rules for the removal of legal effects of re-privatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate, issued in violation of the law, was adopted at the 55th meeting of the Sejm and is aimed primarily at improving the functioning of the verification commission, as well as at improving the situation of people affected by the so-called. wild reprivatization in Warsaw and which resulted in it being wronged. The amendment adopted by the Sejm, among others. it will enable a complaint to be lodged with the administrative court immediately after the decision has been taken, eliminating proceedings for review by the committee itself. But above all, the amendment will allow to reverse the effects of faulty reprivatization decisions issued in previous years, which have affected tenants who have been working for many years in residential premises in Warsaw real estate. In addition, it requires the City Hall to restore the illegally removed tenants to the apartments occupied by them prior to the issue of the immovable property to an unauthorised beneficiary and to stop the fraud of unduly charged rents or other charges. The new rules will also allow the committee to have access to judicial material which may be necessary in the course of the Commission's activities. In the case of cases pending before a court, the President of the court will only be able to send a copy of the file without having to send the original documentation. The new regulations also provide the Commission with access to up-to-date and complete data and information collected in national registers. In addition, they make it clear that the committee may use data obtained from the Presidents of the courts, prosecutors, governmental and local authorities or their subordinate organisational units to carry out statutory tasks and process them without the knowledge or consent of the data subject. These entries are necessary for the entry into force of 25 May this year. EU data protection regulations, GDPR. The law adopted also eliminates the need to hold a hearing in any case, since it is not always necessary to hold a hearing. The Commission may also exclude the disclosure of the hearing, in particular if the details of the family life of the participant in the proceedings are to be discussed. One of the more discussed elements of the amendment adopted is the introduction of an increase in the maximum fine for the party, witness and expert for unjustified non-compliance at the hearing of the committee or leaving the hearing without the permission of the hearing-man - from a sum of 3 000. PLN and 10,000. in the event of a repeated failure to comply with the call for an amount of 10 000. And 30,000. in the event of a repeated failure to comply with the call. The Commission, irrespective of the imposition of the fine, will also be able to request the competent district prosecutor to order the detention and forced delivery of the requested person in cases where it is established that the requested person persistently evades his appointment. The Act will also allow the commission to block the regulations by issuing a warning in the perpetual book already at the stage of the investigation, which will prevent unfair beneficiaries of reprivatization decisions from taking action to prevent the return of the property to the city as a result of the decision taken by the committee. On the other hand, in the case of actions involving harassment of tenants, the amending law will allow the committee to impose a fine on the person who committed such acts, or on the person acting on behalf of the legal person concerned by the decision of the committee. This revenue will only be used for the payment of compensation or compensation to tenants. The Senate has examined this law amending the Law on specific rules for the removal of legal effects of reprivatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate, issued in violation of the law and certain other laws at its meeting on 19 January this year. and accepted five amendments. The Senate shall decide in its first amendment on the status of the members of the verification committee. In the Sejm, this matter was widely discussed at the committee meeting, but it was not resolved, we did not introduce the relevant provisions to the amendment. The Senate accepted that the members of the committee would treat themselves as employees. Thus, they will be included in the catalogue of entities covered by compulsory social insurance as referred to in Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 Act on the social security system and, consequently, health insurance. In addition, this amendment concludes that the payer of social security and health insurance contributions for the members of the committee is the Minister of Justice's office. The other amendments of the Senate aim to eliminate possible interpretation doubts and are more precise. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Legislative Commission at its meeting on 25 January 2018 considered the Senate resolution and requested that all amendments be accepted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-01-25-sejm-57-1.u612	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! It's 24 o'clock, you can make a statement, because it's a special anniversary for the local community that I'm pleased to represent. It is about Mr Edmund Marczak, who is one of the most famous people taking part in the cultural life of the municipality and the town of Nowe Skalmierzyce and one of the most recognizable cultural figures in the Ostrowski district in Wielkopolska. At the same time he is probably the last surviving member of the representative orchestra of the Capital Battalion from the pre-World War II composition. Today he celebrates his 100th birthday. He was born on January 26, 1918 in Duisburg, Germany. Two years later, in 1920, parents moved to reborn Poland and lived in Nowe Skalmierzyce in Ostrów County. He lost his mother early. He's had musical interest since he was a kid. As early as 1932, at the age of 14, he was admitted to the military orchestra 60. Infantry regiment in Ostrów Wielkopolski. Two years later, he was assigned to the 3 year music conservatory in Katowice. It was the best students in this training that gave power to the remodeled band of the Capital Battalion. By order of 10 April 1937, 19-year-old Edmund Marczak was summoned to Warsaw. From then on, he served as a military force, playing in the Capital Battalion's Representation Orchestra until the outbreak of World War II, specializing in drumming. As a member of the orchestra, he took part in various uplifting events of that epoch: he participated in playing national anthems during official visits, as well as during interstate matches, went with the company for solemn changes worth before the grave of the Unknown Soldier, played at funeral ceremonies of significant people, was in the orchestra during the solemn greeting of the coffin with the remains of St. Andrzej Boboli in June 1938 finally took part in occasional concerts for the audience and recordings in Polish Radio. In September 1939 he was assigned to the Warsaw Defence Command as a bishop. After capitulation, he was taken into captivity, from which he escaped, entering the family's New Skalmierzyc. However, in June 1940 he was arrested and deported to forced labor in Bavaria, where he worked until 1945. After the war, he returned to Nowe Skalmierzyc and found employment on the railroad. Until his retirement in 1978, he worked in various railway plants as an official, the longest in the Railway Works in Ostrów Wielkopolski. Since the '50s. In the 20th century, when wind orchestras began to form at various state workplaces, especially railway companies, Mr Edmund Marczak participated in their creation and actively participated in the game. As an outstanding musician who specializes in playing drums, with pre-war practice of playing in the Capital Battalion's Representation Orchestra, he has always been one of the basics of the rhythm section of the orchestra. It is no wonder that various brass orchestras of southern Wielkopolska ran over time, from the 1950s to the 1990s. The 20th century, about Mr Edmund's participation in their composition. In the post-war period, Mr Edmund Marczak was a member of 10 brass orchestras of southern Wielkopolska, except that there were often periods that he played in two or even three orchestras at the same time. Since 2008, due to age, he has limited his music activities to one orchestra - to this day he remains an active instrumentalist of the Urban and Municipal Dęta Orchestra at CSR in Skalmierzyce. Edmund Marczak, promoted to lieutenant rank in 2004, was honored with the title of Veteran Walk for Freedom and Independence of the Homeland, and also awarded with various medals and badges related to merits for the Polish state, army, culture and local community, from which I will mention only recently received Laur Culture of Ostrowski County for 2017. I would like to thank Mr Edmund Marczak for his social effort to promote musical culture in southern Wielkopolska for decades and to express deep respect for his musical skills. I wish the 100-year-old birthday boy more years of health, [[♪ The bell ♪]] strength to music in the City-City Orchestra of the OSP in Skalmierzyce and God's blessing. God bless you, Mr. Edmund!

January 30th this year A thousand years have passed since the conclusion of the peace treaty between Poland and Germany, which is a proof of the power of the Polish state and confirms its independence from external forces. Emperor Henry II, unable to deal with Bolesław Chrobre with his own forces, gained an ally in the form of Kiev's Rusa. Poland suffered a simultaneous attack from the west and east. The year 1017, however, became history as the year of the huge triumph of Polish weapons. The aggressors from across the eastern border were defeated by an army commanded by Chrobry. Before the next anti-Polish war expedition of the German Empire, the Western border was able to protect the population of the western border of our country to a large extent. The heroic defense of Silesian castles, Głogowa and Germans, has gone to history. The invaders gave up the news of defeating their Russian ally and coming the victorious army of Bolesław Chrobre. A thousand years ago, in January 1018, the German Empire troops not only withdrew for Odra and Nysa, but also left the Slavic Lusatia and Milsko. The defeated German ruler himself proposed peace, which was concluded on 30 January 1018. The signed treaty confirmed Poland's membership of all Silesia and Lubuskie land, but also the neighbouring lands of Lusatia and Milska. The integrity and total independence of the Polish state were confirmed. The Room of Węgszyński decided on a long and extremely fruitful Śląska belonging to Poland. Since then, it has remained within the borders of the Polish state for three consecutive centuries and has been a region of flourishing Polish culture. The roots of Witelon, the first great Polish scholar, of European fame at Italian universities, noted as Wratislaviensis - master of Polonorum, are Legnicko-Wroclaw. A quarter of a century before Marco Polo's expedition, the first European expedition to the Far East, led by Benedict Pole, author of the first dictionary of Eastern languages in the history of world science, the first creator of Oriental philology, began from Wrocław. In 1270 the first sentence was written in Polish on the land saved by the Treaty of Wroclaw, Henryków. Peace in Budziszyn was the result of the biggest Polish political victory in Polish-German history. We need to remember and remind. Thank you. [[Applause]]
